- Outsider-to-pull delays (v5_delays.json): v3 105 cases, median 9 blocks, 98 within 20; v5 59 cases, median 16, all within 30; v2.433 89 cases, median 33, only 43 within 20 (bimodal)
- Post-census window (v6_post.json): 1,180 pools in ~6.9 h; v3.0 23.9/h, v2.433 14.7/h, PM2276 10.9/h, v5.0 10.2/h, OURS-orig 2.6/h (18, all nonce 0); engine rule on 180 gate-passing pools +0.30% (24 positive); OURS-orig 17 pools +3.17% (12 positive); gates fee 755 / no_trig 670 / liq 476 / hooks 222 / late 42
- Pricing constants used throughout: stake 0.0012 ETH, gas 4.2e-6 ETH per round trip, TP +1%, MIN trigger 0.0002 ETH, ARM 6 blocks, trigger window 150 blocks, liquidity gate 1e21, block 0.103 s (analyze.py, variants.py, vprice.py)

**Discrepancies with the handoff (the reader's claims; the critic verified the first four areas' ones)**

- HANDOFF 6.5 says operator A's two families (OURS-orig, OURS-copy) 'are the only ones where it prices positive at a 2-block hold'. families.json, variants.json and verify/v2_price.json all price OURS-copy NEGATIVE at hold 2 (-0.32%, 1 of 49 positive; it is positive only from hold 10: +0.22%, hold 20 +2.24%), while PM2276 is nominally POSITIVE at hold 2 (+0.44%, 13 of 164 positive, though -0.13% without its top 5 pools). The defensible statement is: OURS-orig is the only family robustly positive at hold 2.
- HANDOFF 6.5 names `census.jsonl` as the census result; it is not in the repository (nowhere under research/session_oct9_10/others/, not copied per the README's 1.5 MB rule), and none of the cache_*.pkl files are either. The orchestrator's premise that 'results are in the JSON/TXT files beside the scripts' holds for families/crowd/crowdtrace/variants/sigsweep and the ctl/verify JSONs but not for the per-pool census itself.
- HANDOFF 6.5 says fetch.py pulled the pools' 'crowd transactions'; fetch.py fetches only Initialize logs, launch txs, receipts, code hashes and pool logs. The crowd (buyer) transactions are fetched by crowd.py / crowdtrace.py / sigsweep.py into cache_crowdtx.pkl (and by ctl/famfetch.py, verify/v1_fresh.py, v3_fetch.py).
- HANDOFF 6.5 does not mention others/ctl/ (exact feed re-pricing of the 9 Oct 9 live follow trades within 0.13 pp of live; the 94-pool scheme A/B dissection) or others/verify/ (the independent verifier: 26 fresh pools with zero mismatches, independent pricer, 181-pool v3.0 reaction sample, 1,180-pool out-of-sample window), although research/session_oct9_10/README.md indexes both (7 and 16 files). Not a contradiction, an omission.
- HANDOFF 13 says operator A's 'crowd first buy +40..60 blocks (original launcher)'; families.json gives the OURS-orig trigger p10/50/90 as +43/+53/+76 and famanalyze.json scheme-A triggers run +38..+76 (one at +214), so the upper end is ~+76, not +60. Minor.
- HANDOFF 10.2's Oct 9 'result' percentages (e.g. 10:48:51 -0.2%, 10:50:00 +0.1%, 10:50:50 +3.9%) equal the GROSS returns in ctl/ctl_follow.json (-0.17%, +0.11%, +3.90%); the net-of-gas returns are 0.35 pp lower (-0.52%, -0.24%, +3.55%). The $ column is after gas, so the table mixes gross % with net $. Minor unit nuance, not a numerical error.
- HANDOFF 6.6 says 830 of 882 v3.0 pools 'had an outside buy' with median delay to the pull 10-13 blocks; the verify files in this area find far fewer outsider buys under a stricter definition (17 of 181 sampled pools with an outsider >= 0.0002 ETH before the pull in v3_analyze.json; 105 of 717 pools with an outsider of 0.0002-0.02 ETH that were then pulled in v5_delays.json), with the same ~9-10 block median delay. The 830 figure must include the dust wallet 0x93b07902's buys (handoff 6.6 says it is in 792 pools); the two are reconcilable only with that reading.

**Scripts that do not run as copied**

- Every script in others/ except fetch.py reads others/census.jsonl (written by analyze.py) and others/cache_inits.pkl / cache_txs.pkl / cache_rcs.pkl / cache_codes.pkl / cache_plogs.pkl (written by fetch.py); none of these are in the repository. Regenerating them means running fetch.py against the public RPC (Initialize scan over 1.36 M blocks + 4,108 txs + 235 getLogs groups, ~5-10 min) and then analyze.py.
- others/analyze.py and others/variants.py hardcode sys.path.insert(0, "/home/user/v2pons/src") for v4math (buy_out_int, sell_out_int, active_liquidity); fine in this checkout, wrong on any other box. mintrig.py and seat1444.py exec the head of variants.py and inherit the same dependency.
- others/analyze.py reads ../oct8_pools.json (present: 3,192 pools, kinds other 3092 / orig 51 / copy 49) and ../chain.py (present); chain.py needs the eth_utils package (installed here, 6.0.0) and the RPC https://rpc.mainnet.chain.robinhood.com.
- others/crowd.py, crowdtrace.py, sigsweep.py read/write others/cache_crowdtx.pkl (missing) and fetch transactions from the RPC; crowdtrace.py also runs ~7 getLogs calls per 25 wallets over 1.36 M blocks (Transfer logs from the PoolManager). sigsweep.py also needs crowdtrace.json (present).
- others/ctl/ctl_follow.py, famanalyze.py, famfetch.py, live.py hardcode SCR = "/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-v2pons/f653fbde-aa08-514f-a7a0-470737d59aa4/scratchpad" (must become research/session_oct9_10, as the README says); ctl_follow.py also hardcodes /home/user/v2pons/src and imports yday/follow.py (present) and needs cache_ctl.pkl (missing; re-fetch = 157 full blocks x 10 pools + receipts). famanalyze.py needs ctl/cache_famtx.pkl (from famfetch.py, missing) and ../money/pots.json (present) with keys pots['A_7.01']['crowd'] and pots['B_4.86']['crowd']. live.py uses the relative paths ../census.jsonl and live_relay.json, so it must be run with cwd = others/ctl/.
- others/verify/*.py resolve chain.py relatively (OK) but need ../census.jsonl, ../cache_plogs.pkl, ../cache_inits.pkl, ../cache_crowdtx.pkl plus their own verify/fresh.pkl, cache_v3tx.pkl, cache_post.pkl, cache_v7.pkl (none copied). v3_analyze.py, v4_pm2276_burst.py and v5_reaction.py exec the head of v2_price.py (which loads the caches at import); v7_fetch.py has no analysis script and its results were never saved.
- Print-only scripts whose results exist nowhere in the repo: mintrig.py, seat1444.py, verify/v4_pm2276_burst.py, the reaction-adjusted paper of verify/v5_reaction.py, the summary lines of crowd.py / famanalyze.py / v3_analyze.py / v6_analyze.py (including v6's 'recount equal to census set' check).
- analyze.py's output census.jsonl is large (5,164 rows with nested paper dicts; not copied), so a re-run also needs disk and the full fetch first; variants.json/families.json cannot be regenerated without it.

**Open questions**

- Did the independent 50,000-block recount of the census window (verify/v6_fetch.py) equal the 5,164-pool set? v6_analyze.py prints the comparison but v6_post.json does not store it; no file records the answer.
- Where does our buy land relative to the PM2276 burst, and how many PM2276 trigger txs carry ETH as tx value (what the engine sees)? verify/v4_pm2276_burst.py computed it but saved nothing; PM2276's +1.97%/+4.06% with a 0.01 ETH trigger (+1.00%/+2.63% without the top 5) is the only non-operator-A family with a positive engine-style paper, and it was never followed up in the handoff.
- What did v5_reaction.py's reaction-adjusted fixed-entry paper give for v3.0/v5.0/v2.433 (our buy treated as an outsider that triggers the pull 9-16 blocks later)? Only the delays were saved (v5_delays.json); the numbers that presumably killed the PM1444 hold-30 idea are not in the files.
- 12 of the 17 outsider buys in the v3.0 sample (v3_analyze.json) are 0.0004 ETH through the Universal Router with 1,124-byte calldata, the same calldata shape as operator A's crowd; is that operator A probing the volume bot's pools (and getting pulled on), or ordinary UR users? crowd.py's signature test also requires the 0.02 gwei priority fee, which v3_analyze did not check.
- The 'no other follow operator' finding rests on crowd samples of 10 pools in 30 families (those with >= 3 pools having real buys) plus the first two real buys of 2,208 pools; the 595 families with fewer than 3 such pools were covered only by the two-buy sweep. Were any of the small positive outliers (ca11bde0 multicall3: +1.9% at hold 10-30 on 19 pools, 0 pulled; percontract:12700 0xac8d3a09: +23% on 1 pool) ever examined?
- families.json lists 2 OURS-orig pools fee-gated (fee >= 1%, paper +1.17% on those 2) and 6 with no trigger; which launches were these and does operator A sometimes launch at >= 1% (the engine would skip them)?
- variants.json/v2_price.json show OURS-orig hold 2 at +2.9% but hold 5-10 at +3.6% with 1-2 extra pulls of 88 (-100% each); the engine stayed at hold 2. Was the hold extension rejected because of the Oct 9 evening 5-6 block pulls (handoff 10.2) rather than this census?
- crowd.json counts 7 of OURS-orig's 352 sampled buys as our own relay's (to 0x2abb5653, 260-byte calldata); crowd.py does not exclude the relay as analyze.py does, so 'share_ours_crowd 1.000' for OURS-orig is by construction and slightly inflated.
- live_relay.json's head (84,265,072, Oct 9 ~15:40 UTC) shows only 10 Oct 9 trades while handoff 10.2 lists 28 for Oct 9; the remaining 18 came after 16:26 UTC, so ctl_follow's within-0.13-pp verification covers only the morning trades, not the evening ones where the pull came 5-6 blocks after our buy.
- PM1444 v2.433's census paper is negative on the follow rule (-1.77% hold 2, 44 pulled at hold 30) and the hold-30 6-hour buckets swing from -17% to +7%; the front strategy (launch+2, sell on the bot's buy) came from explore/ and dry1/, not from here. Does the bimodal outsider-to-pull delay of v2.433 (half within ~15 blocks, half 100-2,600) correspond to the 'quick-pull mode' hours of handoff 6.10?

## vbot

research/session_oct9_10/vbot/ (57 root files + go/ 2 + react/ 32 + review/ 4 = 95 files, matching the README's counts) is the Oct 9 study of the "volume bot" launcher (PositionManager multicall 0xac9650d8, 1,444-byte calldata) centred on its v3.0 family (3.0 ETH, fee 1%, ts 200, 718 census pools Oct 8 00:00-Oct 9 14:48 + 164 'new' pools to Oct 9 21:28 = 882), with v5.0 (389+70) and v2.433 (531+99, the later front family) as sisters; it is the work that rejected a v3.0 "hold-30 follow" trade and built the calldata recognisers the engine's front later used. launch_decode.py decodes the launch from calldata alone (generic ABI walk + fixed-offset fast path) and launch_decode.json shows 718/718 v3.0 and 1,249/1,249 other PM1444 launches decode and match Initialize/ModifyLiquidity logs with 0 mismatches (1,967 PM1444 total; of 1,419 other POSM launches 368 decode, 1,048 are not multicall+initializePool, 3 are multi-pool txs), and every one of the 718 v3.0 launches has the same sqrtPriceX96 679394651928963067279918579724198 (tick 181141), liquidity 25725498245903809899643, range -887200..887200, actions 020d14, owner == sender. fingerprint.json: of 3,338 POSM+ac9650d8 census launches the levels narrow 3338 -> 1967 (1,444 bytes) -> 1967 (layout) -> 1108 (key/full-range; the 390 extra are v5.0) -> 718 at value 3 ETH = exactly the family, 0 false positives; post_check.json confirms 177/177 strict out-of-sample launches (to block 84508711) match pid/token/sqrtP; replay_window.py on two 36,000-block full-block windows armed 21 and 20 launches from the feed with 0 non-family at L5/L6, saw every real buy (1,434 and 1,430) and sell (1,237 and 1,208), saw all 7 and 9 pulls as the deployer's a0f7978d call through the bot router 0x1cbaf24d in the same block, and showed the engine's buys-only price copy is off by a median 40% while adding the Universal Router sell decoder makes it exact in 2,350/2,671 and 2,630/2,638 swaps. sample_feed.json (76 launches with full blocks): all 322 bot-router buys (c1120e3d, 324 bytes, gas 500000, deadline = ts+1200, 106 wallets) decode, their minOut/tokens-out is 0.95 at the median (0.84-0.95), the 206 sells are all UR 24856bc3 1,060-byte exact-in swaps by bot wallets with minOut 1e12 wei, the first real buy is the bot's in 76/76 at +8 (30), +9 (18), +10 (20), and a 2e-6 ETH dust buy by 0xe71a69f4 lands at +3 in every pool; post_feed.json repeats this on 16 post-census launches (79/79 buys, 33/33 sells, 4/4 pulls seen). The reaction census (reaction.py -> reaction_all.txt, reaction_summary.py -> reaction_cdf.json): 882 pools, 0 buy txs missing; the pull is always the deployer's a0f7978d via 0x1cbaf24d; 830 pools have an "OUTSIDE"-class buy but 792 of them are only the operator-side dust wallet 0x93b07902 (0.000024 ETH at median +132, whitelisted), leaving 155 pools with a non-whitelisted outsider (64 with >= 0.0001 ETH) and 727 with none; after a >= 0.0001 ETH outsider buy the pool was pulled in 64/64 cases, median 10 blocks, CDF 5 blocks 15.6%, 10: 56%, 12: 83%, 15: 94%, 25: 97%, 60: 100%, whereas pools with no outsider were pulled by +45 in 1 of 727 and by +400 in 50. replay.py (exact integer feed replay with our buy inserted, the bot's stale 0.95x minOut and its wallets' proportional sells modelled) on 385 v3.0 pools (291 census + 94 new, report_all.txt): the best robust rule T+12/TP 1%/D42 (fire 12 blocks after the first real buy, sell on the next real buy pricing +1%, else at launch+42) gives +3.07% mean, +5.44% median, 337/380 positive, 15 pulled at -100.35%, ideal upper bound +3.38%; stakes 0.012 and 0.03 ETH give +3.4%, but 0.1 ETH gives -2.90% because 294/380 of the bot's own buys behind ours would revert on their stale minOut (0.1 with P(seen) weighting +1.98%); the Oct 9 18-24h slice is -11.0% (9 pulled of 47) against +4.0..+6.5% in the seven earlier 6-hour slices; the reaction-adjusted expectation (our buy treated as a >= 0.0001 outsider under the CDF) is -69%, and every "seat ahead of the bot's second buy" variant is negative (-0.3% to -4.5%). trade_replay.py (feed-side recognition only, 93 launches) agrees: hold-30 +0.58% overall, +3.85% on the 77 census launches but -15.15% on the 16 post-census ones (3 pulled), hold-2 always -2.34% (the two fees). react/ widens to all three families: bot.json gives the bot's schedule (v3.0 first buy median +9 of 0.125 ETH, second +34, a second buy by +40 in 653/709 live pools, 110 wallets each used in ~68 pools, sells 86-96% of a wallet's holding; v2.433 first buy min 0.005 ETH, 5th pct 0.039, median 0.173, max 0.297, second buy by +40 in only 350/477); pulltime.json splits pulls into REACTIVE (an outsider >= 3e-5 within 25 blocks before: v3.0 210 of 879, v2.433 202 of 630) and other (v2.433 non-reactive lifetime median 3,248 blocks); react2 shows v3.0 and v5.0 pull within 25 blocks of a >= 3e-5 outsider in 57/62 and 23/25 census pools while in v2.433 the 0xb002e8a0 buys via router 0x65050a9b are followed by a pull in only 48/203 (that wallet and 0xf6e55480 are the operator's decoys, buying before the bot's first buy in 368 and 382 v2.433 pools); go/prearm_clock.json shows a pre-arm outsider >= 3e-5 in v3.0 only in 9 pools (all 0xfeda1c58, all pulled within 10 blocks of the bot's first buy) and 0 pulls before the bot's first buy in any family; chains.json shows each of 15 deployers funded by a single >1 ETH transfer just before launch and emptied after the pull; names2.json shows token names recycled across families. review/ holds four reviewer scripts (reaction thresholds, break-even reaction probability, pre-arm checks) whose outputs were not saved. Conclusions carried forward: v3.0 rejected (reaction + a stake ceiling from the bot's stale quote), v5.0 "a trap", and the launch/bot-buy/UR-sell/pull decoders reused for front on v2.433.

**Discrepancies with the handoff (the reader's claims; the critic verified the first four areas' ones)**

- HANDOFF 6.6 says '1,249 of 1,249 PM1444 launches decode and match their receipts'; launch_decode.json counts 1,249 as the NON-v3.0 1,444-byte launches only; with the 718 v3.0 launches it is 1,967/1,967 PM1444 launches (plus 368 of 1,419 other POSM launches by the generic decoder, 1,048 not multicall+initializePool, 3 multi-pool txs).
- HANDOFF 6.6 says '830 pools had an outside buy'; reaction_all.txt's OUTSIDE class counts the operator-side dust wallet 0x93b07902 (792 pools) and the wallets routing through 0x65050a9b that HANDOFF section 1 (line 91) itself calls decoys (0xb002e8a0 18 pools, 0xf6e55480 27); reaction_cdf.json shows only 155 pools with a non-whitelisted outsider and 64 with one >= 0.0001 ETH. The 'median 10-13 blocks' matches 10 (>=0.0001) to 12 (any non-WL) to 14.5 (census/any); 13 appears nowhere.
- HANDOFF section 1 table (line 91) describes 0x93b07902 as '9.5e-5 ETH at about +110 (in 792 pools)'; reaction_all.txt / react2_*.json show 0.000024 ETH (1,246 of 1,423 buys; range 1e-5..1e-4) at median +132 (p10 +114, p90 +159).
- HANDOFF 3.2 and 13 say the bot's router buys are '0.05-0.275 ETH (0.0138 once)'; react/bot.json (v2.433 census, Oct 8-9) already shows first-buy min 0.005 ETH, 5th percentile 0.039, max 0.297, all-buys min 0.005 - the 0.00495-class buy that caused the two -100% trades (section 16.1) was in the Oct 9 data.
- HANDOFF 3.2 says the pull comes '101-139 blocks after launch during bursts; 3,000+ blocks in slow hours'; pulltime.json for v2.433 (Oct 8-9 census + Oct 9 recent) shows non-reactive pull lifetimes q10 1,239, median 3,248, p90 3,685 blocks with only 14 of 428 by +300 - the 101-139 regime is Oct 10's (dry1), not the census's.
- HANDOFF 6.6 says 'v5.0 pulls on outsiders (a trap)'; react2_census.json shows v3.0 is equally reactive (57/62 pulled within 25 blocks of a >=3e-5 outsider vs 23/25 for v5.0); the family that does NOT react to the (decoy) pre-bot buys is v2.433 (48/203). The files do not single v5.0 out.
- HANDOFF 6.8/13 state the bot's minOut = 0.99 x the launch-state quote (v2.433); every vbot script (quotelag*, stale*, replay.py, sample_feed.json w4/tokens max 0.95) finds 0.95 x a stale quote for v3.0 - the two families differ, and 6.6 does not say so.
- research/session_oct9_10/README.md says every result file under 1.5 MB was copied; vbot/reaction.json (the per-pool rows that reaction_summary.py, review/r1_reaction.py and review/r2_adjusted.py read), vbot/names.json and vbot/react/react.json are absent, and others/census.jsonl (5,164 lines, needed by almost every vbot script) is absent from others/.
- HANDOFF 6.6 does not mention trade_replay.json's post-census result (16 launches -15.15%, 3 pulled) nor reaction_census_partial.txt being a superseded partial run (3,925 missing txs) whose numbers (465 of 718, 26 pulled within 20) should not be quoted.

**Open questions**

- How much of the >=0.0001 ETH 'outsider reaction' CDF (n=64, median 10 blocks, used to price the v3.0 rule at -69%) is the operator probing its own pools rather than third parties: 0xb002e8a0 and 0xf6e55480 route via 0x65050a9b (called the operator's decoy in HANDOFF section 1), 0xfeda1c58 (via 0xf0b7eaaf, 9 pools in 2 minutes 16:48-16:49), the 0xd54bad00 burst of 17 fresh-wallet buys with nonces 20..87 in 20:48-20:50 followed by pulls 3-16 blocks later, 0x0ed4dfff's ten 0.0005-0.003 ETH buys 15:26-16:18. The same 0xb002e8a0 buys in v2.433 are NOT followed by pulls (48/203), which argues the v3.0 'reactions' may coincide with quick-pull hours (reactive pulls cluster at 12, 16, 20 UTC in pulltime.json).
- Why do v3.0/v5.0 pools get pulled within ~10 blocks of a non-dust buy while v2.433 ignores its pre-bot decoy buys entirely (0 pulls before the bot's first buy, 9 of 387 within 10 blocks after)? Live Oct 10 front saw the pull 2-3 blocks after our sell (HANDOFF 6.11): reaction to the sell, or the quick-pull regime?
- The bot's stale 0.95x quote (3-14 blocks, PSEEN table) is a v3.0 finding; the engine's FRONT_AHEAD_MAX_ETH logic relies on 0.99x at the launch state for v2.433 (explore/chk, 49 pools). Was the v2.433 quote model checked on as many buys as the v3.0 one (322 here)?
- review/r1-r5, react/react.py, pulls.py, midlife.py, ghost.py, fundcheck.py, chains.py text, breaker.py, sells_fixed.py, stale*.py, quotelag*.py, explore.py, recent_pulls.py, names.py have no saved outputs; their conclusions survive only in docstrings (prearm.py: 9/9 v3.0 pre-arm cases pulled 3-10 blocks after the bot's first buy; vbot_feed.bot_pull: 10 of 10 pulls remove the whole position). Were the reviewer runs (r1-r5) ever summarised anywhere?
- Two deployer provisioning schemes coexist (nonce-3 fresh wallets vs nonce 254-262 reused wallets for v3.0; nonce 3 vs 10/11 for v2.433; 225-234 for v5.0) and chains.json shows one >1 ETH funder per deployer with hop 2 outside the window: who funds the funders, and does the scheme predict pull timing?
- pulls.json shows the 164 post-census v3.0 pools pulled much earlier than the census ones (33 of 154 by +45 vs 7 of 700): did the operator's v3.0 behaviour change on Oct 9 afternoon (consistent with the -11% 18-24h slice), and did it carry into v2.433 (the quick-pull hours seen Oct 10)?
- Relevant to the user's request to remove the front daily cap: the vbot data put the v2.433 launch rate at ~328/day (census) to ~355/day (Oct 9 evening), so a 400 cap binds on busy days while 1,000 never would; nothing in vbot bears on the cap beyond that rate.
- sample_feed shows 1 of 76 launch blocks contained a second family launch; the engine holds one position slot - how often do two front launches share a block on busy days (lost trades)?

## explore (research/session_oct9_10/explore: the 33,984-rule search on 24 families, the front rule's exact replay in adv/ and chk/, the v5.0 traps)

The explore directory (82 files: 31 at the root, 19 in adv/, 17 in chk/, 15 in v5/) is the Oct 9 rule search that produced the live front strategy. prep.py compacts the others/ census (Oct 8 00:00 to Oct 9 14:48:09 UTC, blocks 82878144-84238619, 38.8 h) into pools.pkl; sim.py prices a grid of 72 entries (fire at launch+K for K=2..40, or at the first buy >= 0.0002/0.001/0.01 ETH plus 0..10 blocks) x 2 stakes (0.0012, 0.012 ETH) x 4 take-profits (1/2/3/5%) x 59 holds (2..60) = 33,984 rules per family with a cheap float single-range pricer (gas 4.2e-6 ETH per round trip, buy lands at F+1 priced at the end of its block, a pull at or before the sell's landing = -100%); rules.py picks the best rule per family and stake on the first half by launch time (MID_H 19.4 h) by two criteria (mean, t-stat) and reports it on the second half and per 6-hour slice; 24 families with >= 30 pools, 815,616 rule-tests in all. The rule that became front is, for the v2.433 family (531 pools, 253 train / 278 test, 328 launches a day), "launch+2 | TP 5% | H 19", chosen by both criteria at both stakes: train +11.84% (n253), test +13.01% (n278), median +13.79%, 98.2% positive, 7/7 slices positive (worst slice +10.97%), 1 pulled test pool (-100.35%), exits tp 255 / deadline 22 / pulled 1; at stake 0.012 train +12.07% / test +13.22% / median +14.00%. summary.json shows the rule is flat in TP (13.0-13.4%) and in hold from H10 (+12.47%) to H60 (+14.09%) (H5 +7.01%, H2 -0.84%), flat in fire block K2..K5 (+13.0 to +13.06%), then collapses: K6 +11.67%, K7 +3.57%, K8 +0.82%, K9 -2.83% (the bot buys at +7..+10), and launch+10 TP5 H20 prices -0.97%. The union of all hookless pools with fee <= 1% (2,919 pools) gives "launch+3 TP5 H60" +5.21% train / +6.13% test (7/7 slices) and "launch+5 TP5 H10" +3.77% / +4.04%; v3.0 (717) "launch+2 TP5 H55" +8.09/+8.86% but its forward test has 9 pulls in 166 (+3.46%); v5.0 (389) "launch+3 TP5 H60" +6.14/+5.87% looks clean on logs alone; operator A's own family (94) gives launch+2 TP5 H59 +6.55/+6.94% and the follow-like firstbuy>=0.0002+0 TP1 H2 +2.15/+2.97%. report.json's survivor test (test n >= 30, mean/median/ex-top3 positive, top-1 share < 50%, majority of slices positive, exact replay positive) passes 54 of 80 rule entries; the PM2276 fee-100 family (median -0.35%, forward -0.45% with 8 pulls in 76) and the small PMdirect sub-families fail. The forward test (fwd_fetch/fwd_eval, 1,191 ETH pools launched in the 7 h after the window, 103 of them v2.433) holds the frozen v2.433 rule at +12.53% on 102/102 pools, median +11.75%, 100% positive, 0 pulled. adv/advreplay.py is the exact feed replay (every transaction of blocks b0..b0+45, engine copy with BUY_SLIP 3%, integer v4 math, later swaps re-executed by input, the bot's router buys c1120e3d reverting under their calldata minOut, pulls, gas 103,900 x 2 x base fee) on 24 pools (12 in-window, 6 forward, 6 fresh; replay error <= 2.9e-5): X 0.0012 fire K0..K4 land+1: mean +13.83%, median +14.23%, min +1.17%, 24/24 positive, bot reverts 0; X 0.006 +14.07%; X 0.012 +14.04%; X 0.013 -0.53% on every pool because the bot's first buy reverts 24/24; firing at K >= 6 with a 2-block landing makes 10-12 of 24 of our own buys revert (mean +5.8%); 'sells' view and 'strict' minOut mode change nothing; the cheap pricer agrees with the exact replay to 0.0005. adv/popreplay.py runs the same trade logic from logs on all 633 pools (531 + 102 forward) with the bot's first buy given minOut = floor(0.99 x the launch-state quote): K2 train +11.84 / test +13.01 / fwd +12.53 / all +12.46% (tp 583, deadline 49, pulled 1); the stake sweep 0.004-0.012 ETH is flat at +12.5%, 0.0125 ETH makes the bot revert in 254/633 pools (+8.60%), 0.013 in 620/633 (-0.84%); a competitor's 0.0105 ETH ahead of our 0.006 reverts the bot in 621/633; TP 1% +12.62%, H5 +6.32%, H10 +11.65%, H40 +12.40% (6 pulled); K5 +12.30, K6 +10.75 (38 buy reverts), K7 +4.72 (269), K8 +2.30, K9 -1.45, K10 -2.62. chk/replay.py is a second, independently written exact replay on 25 pools (12 census, 8 forward, 5 new): calldata F=b0+2 or b0+1, land +1 or +2, sell lag 1 or 2: mean +13.25% (0.0012) / +13.47% (0.012), median +15.19%, min +1.18%, 25/25 positive; arming from the receipt instead of the calldata (F=b0+6) +11.35% with 3 buy reverts, F=b0+6 land+2 +3.66% with 17 reverts, F=b0+11 -0.70% with one -100.4% pull-first; the bot's first buy keeps only 1.00024-1.00073x its minOut with 0.012 ETH of ours ahead. chk/react_all.json measures the operator's reaction to outsiders: in v2.433, 431 outsider first-buys (dust trio excluded), 106 pulled within 15 blocks against 8.5 expected at the base rate, lag quantiles p10/25/50/75/90 = 5/6/8/11/12 blocks, 25 of 26 outsiders >= 0.0002 ETH pulled within 15, and 0 outsiders >= 0.0002 ETH before +12 in any family (nobody real buys before the bot); chk/react_ev.json turns that into the pre-live risk: if the operator treated our buy like those outsiders with probability 25/50/96%, the K2 rule's EV would be -1.49% / -15.44% / -41.11% (P(-100%) 48%, exposure p50 7 blocks), K5 +6.51 / +0.72 / -9.94%; this was the one open question before going live, answered live (6.11: pulls 2-3 blocks after our sell, none before). v5/ shows the v5.0 family is a trap: lags.json finds 65 census pools with a non-UR outsider buy > 2e-5 ETH before any pull, 61 pulled 4..24 blocks later (median 16; the 4 survivors were 3e-5..1e-4 ETH), and v5/replay.py's exact replay on 22 pools gives +6.04% (0.0012) / +6.33% (0.012) without reaction but sells at +27..+64, so P(pull <= sell) = 1.00 and the EV with reaction is -100.35%; the K2 TP1 H49 variant +2.60% has P(pull <= sell) 0.35 and EV -33.8%. tokcode.json shows the v2.433 token is a 1,526-byte contract with one code hash (63ad5291cdef) and one unknown selector 3c130d90, the same unknown selector as operator A's 1,770-byte tokens; v3.0/v5.0 tokens are 5,274 bytes with 12 unknown selectors and a different hash per token. The handoff's "+7-9% at every hold" for v2.433 is wrong (the files say +12.5-14.1% for H10..H60), its "49 pools" are 42 distinct (7 overlap), and its 0.0122-0.0124 ETH / $27 ceiling figures are not computed in any explore file (the files bracket the ceiling between 0.012 and 0.0125 ETH, about $31 at $2570). Nothing in explore runs as copied: every result needs pools.pkl, meta.pkl, grid.npy, fwd_pools.pkl, adv/cache.pkl, chk/replay_data.pkl, v5/cache.pkl or the vbot/others caches, none of which were copied, and 15 scripts carry the old absolute scratchpad or /home/user/v2pons/src paths.

**Discrepancies with the handoff (the reader's claims; the critic verified the first four areas' ones)**

- HANDOFF 6.7 says 'for the v2.433 family "launch+2, TP 5%" prices +7-9% at every hold'. explore/rules.json gives the chosen rule 'launch+2 | TP 5% | H 19' at +11.84% train / +13.01% test (stake 0.0012; +12.07/+13.22 at 0.012), and explore/summary.json by_H on the test half: H10 +12.47%, H15 +12.95%, H20 +13.01%, H30 +13.48%, H40 +14.05%, H60 +14.09% (H5 +7.01%, H2 -0.84%). '+7-9%' matches v3.0 (launch+2 TP5 H55 +8.09/+8.86%) or U:PM1444 all (+8.33/+9.77%), not v2.433. The handoff also omits the rule's hold (H 19), which is what FRONT_HOLD_BLOCKS descends from.
- HANDOFF 6.7 says the best rules 'for the union of all sub-1% hookless pools' are launch+3 TP5 H60 (+5.2/+6.1%) and launch+5 TP5 H10 (+3.8/+4.0%). Those numbers belong to the union 'U:ALL hooks0 fee<=1%' (fee up to and including 1%, 2,919 pools); the strict '<1%' union (U:ALL fee<1%, 1,262 pools) has launch+3 TP1 H52 +6.38/+8.15% and launch+2 TP5 H20 +6.13/+7.39% (explore/rules.json).
- HANDOFF 6.8 says 'the exact replay ... on the first 49 pools with full block data: +13-14% a trade, 24/24 and 25/25 positive'. explore/adv/replay.json has 24 pools and explore/chk/replay.json 25, but 7 pool ids are in both (0x6c47407c, 0x5ba20356, 0x4dd98563, 0x7371d5ab, 0x57f1b340, 0xcaff5415, 0xeeb4b8b1), so 42 distinct pools, not 49. The means are +13.83%/+14.04% (adv) and +13.25%/+13.47% (chk), which fits '+13-14%'.
- HANDOFF 6.8 and 13 give the size ceiling as 'about 0.0122-0.0124 ETH', the bot's 0.05 ETH buy filling at '1.0084x minOut at $5, 1.0068x at $10, 1.0052x at $15, 1.0020x at $25, 1.0004x at $30', and 'about $27 a trade'. None of these figures is computed in any explore file (adv/react.py and adv/quote.py compute the room but their output was not saved; the handoff attributes them partly to dry1/sens.py, which only does the reaction EV). What the explore files do show: popreplay.json 0.012 ETH fine (0 reverts), 0.0125 ETH reverts the bot in 254/633, 0.013 in 620/633; adv/replay.json 0.013 reverts 24/24; chk/replay.json headroom with 0.012 ETH ahead 1.00024-1.00073x. That brackets the ceiling at 0.012-0.0125 ETH, i.e. about $31-32 at $2570, so '$27' is a rounded-down safety figure, not a measured one.
- HANDOFF 6.7 says 'grid.npy 397 MB and pools.pkl, not copied'. Also not copied and needed by the explore scripts: explore/meta.pkl, explore/fwd_pools.pkl, explore/adv/cache.pkl, explore/adv/activity_tx.pkl, explore/chk/replay_data.pkl, explore/v5/cache.pkl, and the neighbouring caches vbot/cache_tx.pkl, vbot/react/cache_tx.pkl, vbot/react/cache_tx2.pkl, vbot/react/react.json, others/cache_plogs.pkl, others/cache_inits.pkl, others/census.jsonl, others/cache_crowdtx.pkl. HANDOFF 6.5 names others/census.jsonl as a result file ('5,164 pools, census.jsonl') but it is absent from research/session_oct9_10/others/.
- research/session_oct9_10/README.md says 'the scripts re-fetch them from the public RPC'. For explore this is not true: prep.py rebuilds pools.pkl only from the others/ caches (which others/fetch.py would have to re-fetch first), and sim.py/rules.py/report.py/summary.py/fwd_eval.py/popreplay.py/react_ev.py all require pools.pkl, meta.pkl, grid.npy or fwd_pools.pkl with no fetch fallback.
- HANDOFF 6.7 says 'chk/ holds the checks of the bot's quote model (quotemodel.py, quotestate.py: minOut = 0.99 x the launch-state quote)'. Those two scripts only print; no output of theirs is in the repo. The only saved statement of the finding is the docstring of adv/popreplay.py ('adv/quote.py: exact on 20/20 sampled pools') and the per-pool headroom column in chk/replay.json.
- HANDOFF 3.2 describes the live engine as firing in the launch block so the buy lands at launch+1, while the researched rule is 'launch+2' = fire when the feed shows b0+2, buy landing at +3 (report.json B_offsets {'3': 531}). The files support the engine's choice (adv/replay.json: K0, K1, K2, K3, K4 all +13.83%, identical to 4 decimals; popreplay K2..K5 flat), but the handoff never says that 'launch+2' in the research and 'launch+0' in the engine are different fire blocks inside the same flat region.
- HANDOFF 6.7 says the forward set is '102 forward' pools (adv/popreplay.py docstring agrees), while explore/after.json counts 103 v2.433 launches in the same 7 hours; one launch was dropped by fwd_fetch.py's completeness filter ([b0, b0+3000] not yet complete). Minor.
- explore/chk/replay_react.json exists but no script in the repository writes it (replay_k.py prints only; react_ev.py writes react_ev.json); the handoff does not mention it.

**Scripts that do not run as copied**

- Absolute old scratchpad path /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-v2pons/f653fbde-aa08-514f-a7a0-470737d59aa4/scratchpad (now research/session_oct9_10) hardcoded in: adv/advreplay.py (SCR, also used to import vbot/replay.py), adv/quote.py, adv/minout.py, adv/fingerprint.py, adv/activity.py, adv/armlag.py (path to yday/seat_rpc_lag_probe.json), chk/replay.py (via SCR), chk/look.py and chk/txform.py (…/scratchpad/explore), v5/who.py. Absolute /home/user/v2pons/src (for v4math) in adv/advreplay.py, adv/quote.py, adv/minout.py, adv/react.py, chk/replay.py, chk/minout2.py, chk/quotemodel.py, chk/quotestate.py. **Open questions**

- Where do the handoff 6.8 ceiling figures (0.0122-0.0124 ETH; 1.0084x minOut at $5 ... 1.0004x at $30; 'about $27 a trade') come from? No explore file computes them; adv/react.py's 'room' percentiles and adv/quote.py's binary search printed them but nothing was saved. The saved files bracket the ceiling between 0.012 ETH (0 bot reverts in 633 pools) and 0.0125 ETH (254/633 reverts), about $31 at $2570.
- Which script produced explore/chk/replay_react.json (the reaction-adjusted exact replay for K 2/4/5, TP 5%/1%, land +1/+2)? No script in the repo writes it; it was probably an edited run of chk/replay_k.py with the react_all lags. Its numbers are consistent with chk/react_ev.json and dry1/sens.py.
- Which v2.433 test pool was the one 'pulled' (-100.35%) under launch+2 TP5 H19 in rules.json/report.json/popreplay.json? report.json's examples only list the three most recent test pools; the pool id of the pull-first case is not recorded in any saved output (the later dry1 census found 7 of 435 pools pulled 9-14 blocks after launch, which is the same phenomenon).
- The pre-live reaction analysis (chk/react_ev.json: 25 of 26 outsiders >= 0.0002 ETH pulled within 15 blocks, median lag 8; EV -1.5% to -41% if the operator reacted to our buy) was the open question before going live; the handoff 6.11 says live pulls come 2-3 blocks after our SELL. Is the operator's reaction to our buy really absent, or does it simply arrive after our sell because we are out within 7-10 blocks (lags p10 = 5)? The live data cannot separate those; a slower sell (H 18 deadline trades, e.g. the two -100% cases) is where the reaction would show.
- The v2.433 token (1,526 bytes, one code hash) and operator A's tokens (1,770 bytes) share the same unknown selector 3c130d90 (tokcode.json); every v2.433 token address ends in ...7777 and every v5.0 token in ...7777 too (activity.json, fresh.json). Does the volume bot use the same token factory or launch panel as operator A? Not pursued.
- adv/fetch.log records 20 pools at head 84490227 but adv/replay.json has 24 (6 fresh, the last at b0 84495440): the fetch was re-run later and the log not updated; which run's cache produced popreplay.json's docstring claim 'exact on 20/20 sampled pools' vs the 24-pool replay is unclear (both give 0 bot reverts at <= 0.012 ETH).
- rules.json's frac_rules_pos_train for v2.433 is 0.467: fewer than half of the 33,984 rules are positive in-sample, and the chosen rule is the maximum of that search. The forward test (102 pools, +12.53%, 0 pulled) and the two exact replays (24 and 25 pools, all positive) are the only out-of-sample confirmations before the live 400 trades; the selection bias of picking the best of 33,984 is not quantified anywhere.
- The OURS-copy family (operator A's Oct 8 copy scheme, 49 pools) has no test half; its launch+2 TP5 H10 +8.95% is train-only and never forward-tested (the scheme stopped). The OURS-orig forward test on 18 launches is weak (+1.90% with 1 pull; the follow-like rule +3.25% on 17).
- v3.0 passes the in-window survivor test (+8.86% test, 0 pulled) yet the forward test shows 9 pulls in 166 (+3.46%) and 6.6's hold-30 replay found 15 pulled of 380: the in-window census under-represents the operator's quick pulls for v3.0. Is the same true for v2.433, whose forward and in-window sets show 1 pull in 633 while the Oct 10 dry-run census (6.10) found 7 quick pulls in 435?
- For the owner's request to remove the front daily cap: the explore files put the family's rate at about 326-353 launches a day (activity.json 639 in 47 h; after.json 103 in 7 h; rules.json 328.4/day) with bursts of up to 31 an hour, so an uncapped front would trade roughly 300-350 times a day at about +12.5% each; the handoff's own answer (16.5/16.6 step 4) is set_kv FRONT_MAX_PER_DAY 1000. Whether the owner also wants the per-strategy FRONT_DAILY_STOP_USD (5) kept as the only brake is not addressed by the research.

## fingerprint: research/session_oct9_10/fp and research/session_oct9_10/fpv (plus their producer scripts fp_d10_30.py, fp_d2_9*.py and chain.py at the scratch root) — the v2.433 launch fingerprint day by day, Sep 10 - Oct 10 2026 (HANDOFF 6.9)

This area is the chain-wide history of the volume bot's "front" launch family (PM1444 v2.433: a PositionManager multicall of exactly 2433328500000000000 wei = 2.4333285 ETH, 1,444-byte calldata, selector ac9650d8, fee 2500, tick spacing 50, hooks 0, sqrtPriceX96 1604517591721945638372359205383840 = tick 198329, full-range mint liquidity 49279426163460954002188 at ticks -887250..887250, c0 = ETH), built to answer whether the fingerprint ever changed and whether anyone else launches look-alikes. Every v4 ETH-currency0 Initialize log from the PoolManager was pulled in 100k-block chunks over four agent ranges plus one skeptic range: d10_30 = blocks 59391955-77007489 (Sep 10 12:09:28 to Oct 1 02:12:08 UTC; 177 chunks, 0 gaps, 102,584 logs, 95,670 distinct txs, all 95,670 fetched, 0 missing), d2_9 = 77007490-82879334 (Oct 1 02:12 to Oct 8 00:02:02 UTC; 59 chunks, 0 gaps, half-split check 0 mismatches, 20,920 logs, 18,393 txs, 0 missing, 1,964 kept rows), recent = 84238620-84540923 (Oct 9 14:49:59-23:19:46 UTC; 1,378 logs, 124 kept), oct10 = 84540924-84849139 (Oct 9 23:25:45 to about Oct 10 08:12; 799 logs, 182 kept, independently re-scanned in 25k chunks: 799 logs again, 799 txs fetched, 182 value-match rows), and the skeptic's fpv/scan.py = 82879335-84852915 (Oct 8 00:02 to about Oct 10 08:18; 20 chunks, 7,356 logs, 6,103 txs, 0 missing txs, 0 missing blocks) which fills the Oct 8 - Oct 9 14:49 gap and re-covers recent and oct10. The handoff's headline is 4,030 family launches from the first at block 73,371,234 (Sep 26, ~20:31 UTC; tx 0xe643a42c…) to Oct 10 08:18 with one single fingerprint; the per-day list in HANDOFF 6.9 (39, 285, 296, 283, 292, 292, 290, 322, 314, 300, 274, 198, 311, 353, 181) sums to exactly 4,030. The four agent files hold 1,221 + 1,964 + 124 + 182 = 3,491 family rows (the figure behind the "3,490 distinct senders of 3,491" claim); the other 539 come only from fpv/scan.py's gap fill (the Oct 8 00:02 - Oct 9 14:49 census segment, 221 of them on Oct 9 and 7 in the 08:12-08:18 tail of Oct 10). What can be re-derived from the repository is the 306-launch window Oct 9 14:49:59 - Oct 10 08:07:25 in fp/recent.jsonl and fp/oct10.jsonl: all 306 kept rows are family rows (every tier-2500/50 or sqrt-match init was a family launch), all 306 decode to the identical 8-tuple (sqrt, liq, fee, ts, lo, hi, hooks 0, c0 0), event fields equal decoded fields in 306 of 306, 306 distinct sender wallets, 306 distinct pool ids and tokens, 0 launches of 2.4333285 ETH with another fingerprint, 0 look-alikes by sqrtPrice, no gap over 3 hours, launch spacing 156-4,003 blocks (median 3,420 blocks on the Oct 9 afternoon, 500 blocks overnight), and a burst hour of 53 launches at 07:00-08:00 UTC Oct 10. The handoff's per-day counts for Sep 26 - Oct 9 14:49, the Oct 6 18:43:57 - Oct 7 08:44:51 pause and the one repeated sender cannot be re-derived from the repository because fp/d10_30.jsonl (46,060 rows), fp/d2_9.jsonl (1,964 rows), fpv/fams.json (3,491 rows), fpv/scan_all.jsonl (7,356 rows), fp/d2_9_rest_tx.json and the log/tx caches were over the README's 1.5 MB copy limit; only their meta files, run logs and the scripts that produced them are present. A side fact from d10_30_meta.json: 46,060 of the 102,584 ETH pool inits of Sep 10 - Oct 1 (45%) were kept as PM1444 multicalls or tier/sqrt/value matches, i.e. the volume bot's other PM1444 families dominate v4 ETH pool creation; 1,226 txs matched tier 2500/50 or the family sqrt while 1,221 were family, so 5 tier-matching non-family txs exist in that range whose details were printed by d10_30_final.py but not saved. The day boundaries file gives 846,421-859,580 blocks per UTC day, i.e. 0.101 s per block. Because the fingerprint never changed, release 7d1f645 made the engine log a changed fingerprint ("fingerprint": "new: …" on the armed event) and trade anyway unless FRONT_PIN=1, with the fingerprint pinned by FRONT_FEE 2500, FRONT_TICK_SPACING 50, FRONT_SQRT_P and FRONT_LIQ defaults. Every script in fp/ and fpv/ carries the dead absolute scratchpad path and most need the RPC or the absent caches; none runs as copied. The checkout is at 4dce46e (four commits after the 3c3f696 the task named) and 46 tests pass, matching HANDOFF 16.6 rather than the task's 44.

- fp/run_recent.py and the root fp_d10_30.py, fp_d2_9.py, fp_d2_9_v2.py use datetime.utcfromtimestamp (DeprecationWarning on Python 3.12, seen in fp/d2_9_v2.log); run_recent.py's batch calls have no retry, so a None result would raise on bts[k['block']].

**Open questions**

- What are the 5 tier-2500/50-or-family-sqrt transactions in Sep 10 - Oct 1 that are not family (1,226 kept txs vs 1,221 family in fp/d10_30_tx.log and d10_30_meta.json)? d10_30_final.py's 'tier rows not family' / 'SQRT == SQ but not family' printout was not saved; the handoff's 'no look-alike by anyone else' implies they are tier-only matches with a different price.
- Which wallet is the one repeated sender among the 3,491 agent-file launches, and were its two launches both canonical? Only derive.py's unsaved output knows.

- What did the 539 fpv/scan-only launches (Oct 8 00:02 - Oct 9 14:49 and the 08:12-08:18 tail) look like — same single fingerprint and fresh senders? The handoff says yes for all 4,030, but scan_all.jsonl is absent.
- How are the 46,060 d10_30 'rows' split by value and fingerprint across the volume bot's other PM1444 families (3.0 ETH, 5.0 ETH, 9.333 ETH, 40 ETH, 2 ETH of HANDOFF section 1)? d10_30_final.py's 'POSM 1444 launches by value/fp' table was not saved.
- Has the fingerprint stayed identical after Oct 10 08:18? Only the box's /var/log/flip/engine.jsonl 'armed' events ('fingerprint': 'known' or 'new: …') can say; FRONT_PIN=0 means a change would be traded, not refused.
- fp/oct10_check.py's console breakdown (fee/ts tiers among the 799 inits, POSM-1444 multicalls with other values) was not saved; only the json totals exist.

## dry1 (research/session_oct9_10/dry1 and dry1/skeptic): the front dry run checked against the chain, the 435-pool census of the 24 h before, the skeptic re-derivation, the live audit of the first 132 front trades, engine_log.txt

The dry1 directory (22 files: 4 scripts + 4 results at the top, 10 scripts + 4 results in skeptic/) is the evidence that was used to switch the front strategy from dry run to live on Oct 10 09:07 UTC and to make the two engine changes 32416c1 (sell on any bot-sized buy) and dca5c72 (18-block hold). Top level: fetch.py pulls from the public RPC every v2.433 launch from block 84856239 (the first dry-run pool, 08:24:57 UTC) to head-300 with 36 full blocks per pool, the launch receipt, every Swap/ModifyLiquidity log of the family to head and the transactions behind them, into data.pkl (not in the repository). replay.py reads data.pkl and replays the release-0.9 front rule exactly as the engine had it at dry-run time (stake 0.0012 ETH, TP 1%, HOLD 12, bot-size 0.04 ETH, bot minOut 0.99, ahead max 0.008, buy slip 3%, gas 103,861 + 103,894 at the landing blocks' base fee) in three models per pool: the dry-run model (virtual buy at the head of b0+2, deadline b0+14), a live 'fast' trade (our buy at the tail of b0+1, sell landing send+1) and a live 'slow' trade (b0+2, send+2); the chain side re-prices every intermediate swap by its own sqrtP shift and drops a volume-bot buy whose output with ours in front would fall under its calldata minOut (word 4). Result (replay.json, 18 pools 08:24:57-08:44:06): all 18 sell on the bot's buy at +8/+9/+10 (7/3/8 pools), fast mean +15.66%, median +14.87%, worst +9.61%, best +22.84%; the engine's dry-run tp_ret matches the chain return within 0.00074 (0.074 percentage points) on every pool; the bot's fill with us ahead is 1.00903-1.00906x its minOut (1.0100x without us) so no bot buy reverts; our buy fills at 1.0308-1.0309x our own minOut; nothing but the operator's two decoys (2e-6 ETH via the Universal Router 0x88767899 and 0.0001188 ETH via 0x65050a9b, both at +2..+4) sits ahead of the bot; pulls at +101..+139 in 15 pools, +3108 and +3541 in two, none by block 84868280 in the last. final.py turns replay.json into final.json (the same 18 rows with a one-line 'why'); sens.py (needs numpy and explore/chk/react_all.json) computes that if the operator reacted to our buy like it reacted to census outsiders (106 lags, all <= 14 blocks; P(lag <= 8..10 blocks) = 0.54-0.74) with probability 0.25/0.5/0.96 the expected return would be -3.0%/-21.7%/-56.2%, breakeven reaction probability about 0.21. engine_log.txt is 17 lines of the box's would_sell decisions (pid[:12], launch block, launch+2 = the dry run's virtual buy block, the sell block = the bot's buy block, tp_ret, the truncated reason 'take profit:'), identical to replay.json's first 17 pools (tp_ret within 0.0001). The skeptic/ directory is an independent re-derivation from raw logs with its own integer swap math: fetch_s.py (launches from 08:24:24 UTC to head, Swap+ML logs, blocks b0..b0+16, launch receipts -> data_s.pkl), fetch_tx.py (the missing log transactions), ana_s.py (recomputes every swap's sqrtP from its input, worst relative error 5e-19; 16 placement variants per pool; agrees with replay.py within 4.1e-6 on every pool and every variant, spread across the 16 variants at most 0.011 percentage points), census_s.py + census_ana.py (the 24 h before the window: 417 family pools b0 84022328-84849838 plus the 18 window pools = 435 in census_s.json, 435 distinct launch wallets), census_dig.py (timelines and the by-hour regimes), census_replay.py (no-reaction replay of the 0.9 rule on all 435: buy+1/lag1 mean +12.77%, median +13.80%, 412 TP, 22 deadline, 1 PULL_FIRST = pool 0x4c09db17 at 02:47:33 pulled +11 before the deadline sell at +14, -100.35%), react_s.py (130 non-bot buys >= 0.0002 ETH before the pull in 105 pools, 70 of the 105 first buys from the operator's own dust wallets 0xb002e8a0/0xf05d0e3f at 0.00021-0.00025 ETH; of the 35 real outsiders 31 were pulled within 15 blocks, median lag 11), funder.py/funder2.py (who funds the 0x65050a9b decoy wallets and the launch wallets; prints only). Census findings: pull-launch median 1506 blocks (q05 28, q25 117, q75 3411); pull - bot buy min 2, q05 19, median 1498; 7 pools pulled 9-14 blocks after launch (six at 02:47:16-02:49:03 UTC, one at 08:13:54), each 3-4 blocks after the bot's own buy; 11 pools pulled within 15 blocks of the bot's buy; the bot bought in all 435 pools (by +10 in 412, by +14 in 422, by +20 in 432; +7..+10 in 409 = 94%), never after the pull; two regimes by hour (slow: launch gaps 3300-3800 blocks and pulls 3100-3600 after launch, about 170-190 blocks before the next launch; bursts at 10-09 11h/16h and 10-10 02h/05h/07h/08h: gaps 198-500, median pulls 38-330). live_front_check.json (no producing script in the repository; made at about 09:58 UTC) holds 136 relay trades from block 84881639 (09:08:46) to 84909316 (09:56:30): 132 front (61 at 0.00195 ETH = $5, 23 at 0.0039 = $10, 48 at 0.0058 = $15) and 4 follow at 0.0012 ETH ($3). Front: pulled before our sell 0 of 132; pull minus our sell 1 block x3, 2 x119, 3 x9, 11 x1; pull minus the bot's buy 2 x3, 3 x121, 4 x8; our buy at launch +1 x130, +2 x2; the bot's buy at +7 x7, +8 x33, +9 x41, +10 x37, +11 x6, +13 x1, +14 x3, +15 x2, +19 x1, +130 x1; our sell at the bot's buy +1 x130 (same block x1 = 0x46a6258e deadline at +14, 8 blocks before x1 = 0xe9be428f stall); returns (gross of gas, from the Swap amounts) mean +14.28%, median +16.25%, 129 of 132 up, worst -0.49%; by stake $5 +14.18% (worst -0.49%), $10 +13.59% (worst +0.76%), $15 +14.74% (worst +0.44%). The bot's size in those 132: min 0.005, 5th pct 0.022, median 0.198, max 0.3265 ETH, 12 under 0.04. The 4 follow rows: buys at launch +41/+45/+51/+62, sells 3 blocks later, pulls at +46 and +56 (5 after our buy), two unpulled. On the handoff's open item 12.1 the dry1 tools are a poor fit: replay.py models the dry-run-era engine (HOLD 12, no sell-on-size, no router rule, stake 0.0012) and needs data.pkl rebuilt by fetch.py from block 84856239; fetch_s.py feeds only the skeptic's ana_s.py; the item was in fact resolved (16.1) with research/session_oct9_10/front_audit/step1_our_swaps.py and step2_full_audit.py, which read our relay's own Swap events. Note for the orchestrator: the repository is at 4dce46e (four commits past the 3c3f696 named in the task), engine tests are 46 not 44, and nothing in dry1 bears on FRONT_MAX_PER_DAY (the cap the owner wants removed); the census's 280-350 launches a day explains why 400 was reached at 11:39 UTC.

**Discrepancies with the handoff (the reader's claims; the critic verified the first four areas' ones)**

- 6.10 says 'the first 18 front decisions of the dry run (Oct 10 08:24-08:38)': replay.json/final.json hold 18 pools from 08:24:57 to 08:44:06; engine_log.txt holds 17 decisions to 08:38:24; the 18th replayed pool 0x0dde73ea (08:44:06) has no engine_log line.
- 6.10 says 'pulls 101-139 blocks after launch': true for 15 of the 17 pulled pools; 0x3d419a341a was pulled +3541, 0xb6c112d949 +3108, and 0x0dde73ea was not pulled by block 84868280 (final.json, replay.json).
- 6.10 says 'over the 435 pools of the 24 hours before': census_s.json's 435 = 417 pools of the 24 h before (b0 84022328-84849838) plus the 18 dry-run window pools (census_ana.py concatenates C['fam'] + D['fam']).
- 6.10 says 'the bot's buy came by launch +14 in 420 of 435 pools': census_s.json gives 422 at <= +14 (two pools at exactly +14); 420 is the count strictly before +14. 'by +20 in 432' matches.
- 6.10 'In one pool the bot bought only 0.0138 ETH ... the pull at +11 comes first': the PULL_FIRST pool in census_replay.json is 0x4c09db17 (b0 84660647, 02:47:33, bot +8, pull +11) but no dry1 file records its bot size; the 0.0138 figure cannot be checked from the repository (0.01353 ETH appears in live_front_check.json for a different pool, 0xe9be428f).
- 6.11 reads the live data as 'the operator reacts to our sell, after we are out', while 16.1/16.2 read the same pattern as 'the pull 3 blocks after its bot's buy, not a reaction to us'. live_front_check.json cannot separate the two: pull = bot + 3 in 121 of 132 and pull = sell + 2 in 119 of 132 because our sell lands at bot + 1 in 130 of 132. The census's quick-pull pools (02:46-02:49, 08:13, nobody else in) favour 16.1's reading; the dry-run window (08:24-08:44, no buy from us, pulls +101..139) leaves open why the regime changed at about 09:07.
- 6.11 / 10.4: the live audit's $5 count is 61 trades (live_front_check.json, and 16.2's full audit also says 61) while the 10:00 results.py screen in 10.4 says '$5 60 trades'.
- 16.2 says the 36 bot buys under 0.04 ETH were 'all sold on the buy for +0.4% to +1.9% except the two at 0.00495': live_front_check.json row 0x111a9d84 (09:34:07, $5) shows a bot buy of 0.005 ETH at +8 sold at +9 for -0.09% (front_audit/full_audit.json has the identical row).
- 12.1 names 'dry1/replay.py and dry1/skeptic/fetch_s.py' as the tools for the two -100% trades: replay.py takes data.pkl from dry1/fetch.py (fetch_s.py's data_s.pkl feeds only the skeptic's ana_s.py and has a different layout); replay.py models the engine as it was at the dry run (HOLD 12, no FRONT_SELL_ON_ETH rule, no FRONT_BOT_ROUTERS rule, stake 0.0012, dry-run buy at b0+2) so it would not reproduce the live engine of 10:49/11:12 (HOLD 18, sell-on 0.005, stake 0.0058) without edits; fetch.py would have to be re-pointed (FROM 84856239 -> near 84939677/84953010) or it fetches every family launch since 08:24. The item was in fact resolved (16.1) with front_audit/step1_our_swaps.py and step2_full_audit.py.
- Task framing (not the handoff): the branch head is 4dce46e, four commits past the 3c3f696 named in the task, and the engine's test count is 46 (the task said 44).

**Scripts that do not run as copied**

- All 10 skeptic/*.py scripts hard-code SCR = /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-v2pons/f653fbde-aa08-514f-a7a0-470737d59aa4/scratchpad for `from chain import ...`; it must be changed to research/session_oct9_10 (README says so) or every one fails at import.
- replay.py, skeptic/ana_s.py and skeptic/census_replay.py insert /home/user/v2pons/src for v4math (absolute; fine only where the repo is at that path). replay.py and fetch.py import chain.py from the parent directory relative to __file__ (works as copied).
- replay.py needs dry1/data.pkl, which is not in the repository (pickles were not copied); rebuilding it means running fetch.py against the RPC, which now scans Initialize logs from FROM=84856239 to head-300 and fetches 36 full blocks for every family pool since 08:24 Oct 10 (hundreds of pools) plus all their logs and transactions: hours of RPC time unless FROM/SCAN are edited.
- final.py opens 'replay.json' and writes 'final.json' by relative path: must be run from the dry1 directory.
- sens.py opens '../explore/chk/react_all.json' and 'replay.json' by relative path (run from dry1) and needs numpy; react_all.json exists in the repository.
- skeptic/ana_s.py, census_ana.py, census_dig.py, census_replay.py, react_s.py need data_s.pkl (from fetch_s.py) and census_s.pkl (from census_s.py); react_s.py also uses txs_react.pkl as a cache; funder2.py writes blk_862.pkl and opens 'data_s.pkl' from the CWD; none of these pickles are in the repository.
- fetch_s.py's window is 'from 08:24:24 UTC Oct 10 to head': a re-run returns a much larger family set than the 18 pools analysed, so the downstream JSONs would not be reproduced as they are; census_s.py derives its 24 h window from data_s.pkl's start.
- chain.py requires eth_utils (pip install eth-utils) and reaches https://rpc.mainnet.chain.robinhood.com; census_s.py, census_replay.py, react_s.py, funder.py, funder2.py, fetch_s.py, fetch_tx.py, fetch.py all make RPC calls (the task forbids re-running them).
- engine_log.txt's columns are undocumented and the last column is cut after 'take profit:' (the sell reason is lost).
- live_front_check.json has no producing script anywhere in the repository (grep finds it only in docs/HANDOFF.md); its 'pid' is 10 characters, and 'ret' is not labelled (inferred gross of gas).

**Open questions**

- Is the live pull-at-bot+3 pattern an operator regime that happened to start at about our go-live (09:07) or a response to our presence? The census shows the same quick-pull behaviour with nobody else in the pool at 02:46-02:49 and 08:13 UTC, but during the dry-run window itself (08:24-08:44, no buy from us) the pulls came +101..+139; nothing in dry1 settles it.
- ana_s.json pool 0x0dde73ea: the skeptic's validator stopped at a Swap log whose fee field read 2899 in a static 2500 pool (hooks 0); unexplained, not mentioned in the handoff.
- The bot's 0.005 ETH buy in 0x111a9d84 (sold at -0.09% at +9): is 0.005 a distinct bot size from the 0.00495 that caused the two -100% trades, or a rounding in the audit's bot_eth? It matters for whether the FRONT_SELL_ON_ETH 0.005 threshold was ever exactly at the bot's size.
- The 0.0138 ETH bot buy claimed for the census's one full-loss pool (0x4c09db17) is unverifiable from the repository (census_s.json carries no sizes; the pkl caches were not copied).
- live_front_check.json field semantics are inferred: 'ret' gross of gas from the Swap amounts (the two deadline trades read -0.4893% = two 0.25% fees), 'bot' for the 4 follow rows apparently the first >= 0.04 ETH crowd buy; no script documents them.
- Why results.py's 10:00 by-stake table counted 60 $5 trades while the chain audit (09:58) counts 61 at 0.00195 ETH.
- funder.py / funder2.py (who funds the 0x65050a9b decoy wallets and the launch wallets) left no recorded output; their findings, if any, are only in the handoff's money/origins sections.
- For the owner's actual request (remove the front daily cap): nothing in dry1 sets or justifies FRONT_MAX_PER_DAY=400; the census's launch rate (280-350 a day, bursts of 30-52 an hour) shows the cap binds mid-day, and 16.6 step 4 proposes 1000; the only exposure bound left would be FRONT_DAILY_STOP_USD (5) and the halt file. Whether the cap had a safety purpose beyond that is the orchestrator's question, not answered by these files.

## yday-protect

research/session_oct9_10/yday/ (41 files + vfy/ 11 + vseat/ 5) is the Oct 9 paper on the operator's Oct 8 launches; research/session_oct9_10/protect/ (76 files + review/ 10) is the follow protection study of Oct 9. yday/follow.py replays flip_engine 0.8's follow entry (feed replay, trigger >= 0.0002 ETH at +7..+150, buy at T+1/T+2 priced after every other swap of its block, minOut = feed quote x 0.97, take-profit 1% after base_fee x 250k gas on feed buys in B+1..B+2, else deadline sent at B+2, sell lands send+1 fast / send+2 slow) on the 51 original-launcher launches of Oct 8: 46 traded (2 skipped fee 10000 ppm, 3 no real buy within 150 blocks); at 0.0012 ETH land1_fast mean +2.19%, median +1.61%, worst -0.84%, 37 of 46 wins, +0.001209 ETH total, 32 take-profit / 14 deadline exits; land1_slow +3.28% (40 wins); land2_fast +2.70% (32 wins, 4 buy_reverted); land2_slow +0.84% (one pull_first at the 13:19:44 launch). Gas: buy 103,861, sell 103,894, sell into a pulled pool 135,200 gas (medians of our 8 Oct 8 relay buys / 5 sells / 3 pulled sells), base fee median 0.0202 gwei, round trip $0.0108. stakes_follow.py / follow_stakes.json extends the same pricer to 10 stakes 0.0012..1.0 ETH: land1_fast mean +2.19% (0.0012), +2.43 (0.004), +2.49 (0.012), +2.47 (0.03), +2.41 (0.06), +2.33 (0.1), +2.16 (0.2), +2.01 (0.3), +1.78 (0.5), +1.38% (1.0 ETH, 29 TP / 17 deadline), i.e. returns fall above about 0.03 ETH. An adversarial verifier (vfy/vprice.py, independent code from its own raw fetch) repriced all 552 results within 4.98e-7 with identical exits, and corrected one live record (trade 8 ret_net -0.0973 -> -0.0990 after two approval transactions); follow_verified.json = follow.json + that correction + a verification block. yday/seat.py prices the seat entry (fire at the feed's L+6, buy lands L+7/L+8) on the 49 copy-scheme launches of Oct 8 14:59-17:12: land7_fast +7.92% mean, 44 of 49 wins (44 take-profit, 4 deadline, 1 buy_reverted), land7_slow +8.37% (46 wins), land8_fast +6.70% (10 buy_reverted because the burst at +8 lands before/with us); the 148 crowd burst buys have amountOutMinimum = 90% of their quote and none would fail with our buy ahead at 0.0012/0.004/0.012 ETH; vseat/vprice.py matches seat.json to 9.2e-16 on 588 results; the live RPC receipt-lag probe (231 feed txs) found the public RPC serves a receipt 591/794/1021 ms (p10/50/90) after the feed shows the tx = 5/7/10 feed blocks, so from this container only 16% of seat launches would fire at L+6, 18% at L+7, 66% too late (the box's 0.5 release: 14 of 29 armed too late). yday/reaction.py classifies every buy on the 101 operator launches by the crowd's transaction fingerprint (Universal Router execute, 1,124 bytes, deadline 2^256-1, type 2, priority 0.02 gwei, gas 220k/880k/260k: 4,634 of 4,854 buy txs) and measures the pull after a non-crowd buy: our 7 quick-pull trades of 13:15-13:19 were pulled 5,6,6,6,7,8,13 blocks after our buy (the sell under the current exit lands +3), outsiders on classic launches 11-51 blocks. protect/exits_bt.py takes the verified pricer and tests 8 exit variants plus 2 reference rows with the operator's pull modelled at our buy B+2..B+6 and at the trigger T+3..T+7 (validation against follow.json/follow_stakes.json: 368 prices within 4.98e-7): with our buy at T+1 (fast landing) every variant is -100% at B+2; at B+3 V0/V3/V4d lose 84.7% (39 of 46 pulled first), V4b 82.5%, V2 42.6%, while V1 (deadline at B+1) prices -0.17% at 0.0012 / +0.15% at 0.1 ETH, V4c -2.35% / +0.15%, V4a -4.54% / -4.22%; from B+4 on V0 keeps +2.19% (0.0012) / +2.33% (0.1); with the buy at T+2 the pull at B+3 catches V0 on 17 (0.0012) or 15 (0.1) launches (-35.4% / -30.8%) while V1/V4d keep +1.61% / +1.79%; the slow (send+2) landing shifts every column one block later (V0 safe only from B+5). exits_cadence.out: the crowd's next real buy after the trigger comes at T+3 on 30 of 46 launches and at T+6 on 22 (T+2: 9, T+4: 4, T+5: 8), which is why V0's whole edge over V1 (sum 1.0051 at 0.1 ETH) comes from trades sold on the T+3 buy. measure.py / prot_eval.py / summary.py classify the 862 fingerprint wallets into A (32, the classic crowd), B (31, the 7 low-fee long launches), fresh (798, nonce <= 3) and the speck buyer, and show 88 of 94 original launches had an engine trigger, all 88 from the crowd fingerprint, 11 from fresh wallets (nonce <= 3); the fastest reactions were to us (5-8 blocks on the 13:15-13:19 fresh-trigger launches); outsider real-size buys occur 0.25/h and are followed by a pull within 60 blocks 11 times in 36 h (lags 6..51, none <= 5). ladder_sim.py / ladder_sim2.py simulate a stake ladder (rungs 0.0012 -> 1.0 ETH, WARN at pull <= +6 -> cooldown, HALT <= +4, fresh trigger -> minimum stake): with the ramp done it keeps 65-79% of a flat top stake's profit over the 55 Oct 8-9 trades; residual.py puts the residual cost of an abrupt speed-up at $4.8-20.6/day at 0.1 ETH against $59.9/day of profit. live_all.json / rv3.json re-derive our 18 relay trades Oct 8-9 from the chain (buy/sell/pull blocks, trigger fingerprint and nonce). pulls_feed.json shows all 142 operator pulls are PositionManager calls (selector 0xdd46508f, 676 bytes, 1.2M gas, 0.02 gwei priority) carrying the token address, so the feed can see them.

**Discrepancies with the handoff (the reader's claims; the critic verified the first four areas' ones)**

- HANDOFF 6.3 says 'at 3 blocks V1/V4 variants survive (-0.2% to +1.6%)'. exits_bt.out: at a pull B+3 with the buy at T+1 (fast) only V1 (-0.17% at 0.0012, +0.15% at 0.1) and V4c (-2.35% / +0.15%) avoid the catch; V4a loses 4.54% / 4.22% (2 pulled), V4b loses 82.51% / 79.98% (38/37 pulled), and V4d is identical to V0 (-84.70%, 39 pulled) because its T+3 deadline is B+2 when the buy lands T+1. Only with the buy at T+2 do V1 and V4d both hold (+1.61% / +1.79%), while V4a is still -9.36% / -4.76% and V4b -26.56% / -26.36%.
- HANDOFF 6.3 and 16.3: 'no variant keeps a profit at +3 (the best, deadline at buy+1, prices -0.17%)'. True only at 0.0012 ETH with the buy at T+1: V1 at B+3 is +0.15% at 0.1 ETH (+0.16% at 0.03, +0.10% at 0.3, +0.06% at 0.5, exits_stakes.json) and +1.61%/+1.79% when the buy lands T+2 (0.0012/0.1), as is V4d.
- HANDOFF 6.3: 'at 4+ blocks V0 is best (+2.2 to +2.7%)'. Holds for buys landing T+1/T+2; with the buy at T+3 V0 is -1.44% (0.0012) / -1.25% (0.1) at every pull offset >= B+4 while V1/V4d/V4c are +0.42% / +0.62% (exits_bt.out blocks 3 and 6).
- HANDOFF 6.3: 'nothing survives a pull 2 blocks after our buy (every variant -91% to -100%)'. With the buy at T+3 the B+2 column is -72.13% / -71.74% (33 of 46 caught), and the reference 'sell sent B+0' row survives at -0.31% to -0.78%; the -91..-100% range covers only the T+1/T+2 landings.
- HANDOFF 6.2: 'follow_stakes.json (stakes 0.0012/0.004/0.012: the same returns)'. The file holds ten stakes 0.0012..1.0 ETH and the mean return falls with stake: +2.49% at 0.012, +2.33% at 0.1, +2.01% at 0.3, +1.78% at 0.5, +1.38% at 1.0 ETH (yday/stakes_out.txt).
- HANDOFF 6.2: 'the seat on the 42 copy-scheme launches +8.9% (42 of 42) if the burst still comes'. yday/seat.json and seat_out.txt price 49 copy launches: land7_fast +7.92% with 44 of 49 wins (the 44 take-profit exits average +8.87%), land7_slow +8.37% 46 wins; no 42-launch subset exists in the files (burst >= +9: 39 launches +8.04%; burst == +9: 35 launches +9.01%).
- HANDOFF 6.2: 'validate_follow.py checks the pricer against the live fills: max |ret diff| 5e-7'. validate_follow.py has no saved output; its checks are v4math vs real fills, feed vs chain sqrtP and additivity vs exact replay. The 5e-7 figure is the adversarial verifier's (yday/vfy/vfix.py: 552 results within 5.0e-7) and exits_bt.out's validation (4.98e-7 over 368 prices).
- HANDOFF 6.3: 'react9_*: Oct 9's pulls relative to our buys (the evening pulls at +5/+6)'. protect/ has no react9_* script; fetch9.py writes react9_data.json (not in the repo) and fetch9b.py writes react9b_data.json, which covers only 5 launches of Oct 9 11:19-12:06 UTC (head 84143425). live_all.json ends at 11:36 UTC (head 84142864). Nothing under protect/ or yday/ contains the Oct 9 evening (16:27-22:34) trades or their +5/+6 pulls; the handoff's source for those is results.py (10.2) / the scratch-root exits.py (6.12).
- HANDOFF 6.3 and 12.3 describe the Oct 9 protection proposal as a near-miss halt, a crowd allowlist, a stake per pot and a take-profit floor. protect/ contains a different and larger design never mentioned in the handoff: a stake ladder (ladder_sim.py / ladder_sim2.py: rungs 0.0012 -> 1.0 ETH, RAMP after K clean trades, WARN at a pull <= +6 -> cooldown at the minimum stake for 3 clean trades, WATCH <= +16, HALT <= +4, fresh trigger nonce <= 3 -> minimum stake), the canary census (prot_eval.py: outsider buy -> pull), the residual-risk model (residual.py, review/rv9.py) and exits_stakes.py. None of 'allowlist', 'stake per pot' or 'take-profit floor' appears in any protect/ or yday/ file.
- HANDOFF 10.1 lists trade 8 (14:32:16) at -9.3%; yday/follow.json live ret_net is -0.0973 (gross -0.0929) and follow_verified.json corrects it to -0.0990 after the two approval transactions; the table's other returns are gross figures (trade 1 +7.5% gross vs +7.17% net, trade 6 -0.5% gross vs -0.83% net).
- HANDOFF 16.3 built FOLLOW_NEAR_MISS_BLOCKS = 4 ('a pull at +5 or later is not a near miss'). protect/ladder_sim.py's design classified a pull at +5/+6 as WARN (reset to the minimum stake) and +7..+16 as WATCH; the Oct 8 13:15-13:19 pulls at +5,+6,+6,+6 (margins 2-3 blocks after a sell landing at +3, summary.json) would not trigger the 4-block halt as built.
- research/session_oct9_10/README.md counts protect as 75 files; the directory holds 76 at the top level plus 10 in review/.

**Scripts that do not run as copied**

- 57 scripts under protect/ and yday/ carry the absolute scratch path /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-v2pons/f653fbde-aa08-514f-a7a0-470737d59aa4/scratchpad (for chain.py on sys.path and as HERE/SP constants); it must become research/session_oct9_10 (README says so). - Scripts that are RPC fetchers by design and must not be run here: yday/fetch.py, fetch_fp.py, seat_fetch.py, seat_fetch_deploy.py, funding.py, dustcheck.py, probe.py, calldata.py, vfy/vfetch.py, vgas.py, vcopies.py, vpfee.py, vseat/vfetch.py; protect/fetch9.py, fetch9b.py, launches9.py, live_all.py, exits_live9.py, exits_live9b.py, bynonce.py, scan_funding.py, wallet_logs.py, wallet_profile.py, tokens_origin.py, follow_money.py, precursor.py (fetches blocks), measure.py and classify.mean_block_ms (block timestamps), pulls_feed.py, review/rv2, rv3, rv4, rv7; yday/seat_rpc_lag_probe.py connects to the live sequencer feed through flip_engine.
- Runnable offline after the path edit (inputs present): protect/summary.py (measure.json), ladder_sim.py (yday/follow_stakes.json, prot_eval.json, live_all.json), ladder_sim2.py, residual.py (+ exits_stakes.json), review/rv9.py; yday/vfy/vfix.py needs the RPC for two receipts.

- protect/review/rv3.py writes rv3.json to the current directory; protect/ladder_sim2.out is truncated at 20 lines (the trace it prints is not captured); yday/funding_870072_295877_2b7be2.json is an empty object.

**Open questions**

- Where does the handoff's seat figure '+8.9% (42 of 42)' come from? yday/seat.json has 49 launches, 44-46 wins, +7.9/+8.4% means; the closest is 44 take-profit exits at +8.87%.
- Is FOLLOW_NEAR_MISS_BLOCKS = 4 the right threshold? exits_bt shows V0 is only safe when the pull comes >= B+4 (fast) / B+5 (slow), i.e. a 1-block margin at B+4; the Oct 8 13:15-13:19 pulls at +5/+6 (margins 2-3 blocks) and ladder_sim's WARN rule (<= +6) suggest 6 as the warning level; the built halt ignores +5/+6.
- Was the stake-ladder / cooldown design (ladder_sim2.py: 65-79% of flat profit kept, residual $4.8-20.6/day at 0.1 ETH) ever shown to the owner? The handoff never mentions it, and the follow stake is being raised to $10 without it.
- exits_bt's slow landing (send+2) shifts every safe offset by one block; live sells have landed send+1 so far (live_all: sell B+2/B+3 for sends at B+1/B+2) but the 09:15 Oct 10 sequencer stall shows later landings happen; no variant handles that.
- The canary signal (an outsider's real buy followed by a pull within 60 blocks, 0.14/h, lags 6-51) and the feed-visible pull shape (142/142 PositionManager 0xdd46508f calls carrying the token) were measured but no engine rule consumes them; is a feed-side pull detector worth building for follow?
- Why do returns fall with stake (0.1 ETH +2.33%, 1.0 ETH +1.38%): the handoff's 'same at $3 and $25' holds, but the 1.0 ETH land2_slow total is negative (-0.186 ETH) from a single pull_first; the loss-per-catch at larger follow stakes is not in the handoff.
- The Oct 9 evening pulls at +5/+6 (16:27 onward) that motivated 12.3 are not in protect/ or yday/; the data for them sits only in the box log (results.py) and the scratch-root exits.py (6.12).
- exits_bt.json, react_data.json, react9_data.json, cache_follow.pkl and cache_seat.pkl were not copied; re-running any protect/ pricer requires the RPC. Should the cache files be regenerated once and committed under research/ for offline reproduction?

## reviews: the independent review agents' scratch directories for releases 0.6, 0.8, 0.9 (research/session_oct9_10/review06_*, verify_*, rv, rv09money, vfy_lookalike, vdstop3, vlate, vpnl, score_mix; plus rt/, repro/, chk/ which the README lists as review scratch)

The area holds 36 directories (about 290 files, 190 of them verbatim copies of the repository at c2d9fcb or of the engine/tests at bd8ed8d, 48af392, 506a254, 7d1f645 — every copy diffed clean against git) written by four distinct review passes, none of which kept its verdicts in writing: apart from review06_tests (13 identical 20-line "ok" transcripts), review06_refute_getcode/rg_stdout.txt (one two-line result), review06_lc/ops.txt and review06_refute_getcode/copies.txt (two launcher lists), every result file is missing or 0 bytes, so which finding was confirmed or refuted has to be read off the fix commits' messages and tests. Pass A (verify_engine, verify_live, verify_backtest, verify_adversarial, Oct 9 morning, engine 0.3-0.5 and the Oct 8 research): re-found the 8 live trades from the chain (blocks 83279856-83402530), re-priced them with its own v4 integer math, flagged blocks holding a single transaction as kind-4 feed messages the engine could not see, re-implemented the 0.3 exit backtest with the shadow buy inserted (variants A-J), and modelled P(sell after pull) from the 7 relay pulls {+5:1,+6:3,+7:1,+8:1,+13:1} — its confirmed findings are the kind-4 decoding and the unexercised test paths fixed in c2d9fcb (0.6, 11 -> 20 tests). Pass B (review06_*, on c2d9fcb, finder+refuter pairs) confirmed five engine findings fixed in 5827baf (0.8, 23 tests): the nonce wedge (a lost-reply buy that never went in plus a failed exact nonce refresh leaves the engine at +2 and every later buy "nonce too high"; probe_wedge vs probe_wedge_control), the synchronous buy send blocking the feed/sell/watchdog for the 4+4 s socket timeouts (real_sender_repro with the real Sender against a local sequencer in normal/reset/silent1/silent2 modes), the launcher code read as "0x" from a node a few blocks behind (real_getcode: 4 of 30 empty first reads at a 7-block deploy gap, 0 of 30 at 8 blocks) so a copy launcher was never eligible, the day's only trade consumed by a buy that never went in (probe_cap with MAX_TRADES_PER_DAY=1), and results.py failing or lying on adversarial logs/env/RPC (review06_ops, review06_ops_halt, review06_user's 15 scenarios, rt/). Two Pass-B probes were never fixed in code and have no recorded verdict: reply_kind() raising on a string-valued "error" so deliver_sell dies before settle and the position stays open (probe_strerr, rk.py; reply_kind at HEAD is byte-identical to c2d9fcb and deliver_sell has no try/except), and _oneoff()'s unclosed connection on its error path (leak.py; CPython's GC closes it, so probably refuted). Pass B also stress-ran the 0.6 suite 13 times under setswitchinterval(1e-6)/3 CPU hogs with no failure. Pass C (verify_dstop, vdstop3, vpnl, verify_slot, verify_wd, vfy_lookalike, vlate, score_mix, rv09money, chk/, repro/, on 48af392) confirmed nine 0.9 findings fixed in 506a254 (29 -> 39 tests): the shared daily stop (one pulled front trade at the 0.006 ETH max, -$15.4, stops follow for the day, and front wins mask follow losses), a live front position eating a follow pool's trigger, the watchdog's time fallback selling the wrong (new live) position after a dry front drop, a look-alike launch with a fake sqrtPriceX96 traded at its fake price and a pool id re-armed by a later copy, a look-alike with out-of-range liquidity (liq 0) raising out of ingest and losing the block's follow trigger, FRONT_STAKE_ETH=0 sending the follow stake with minOut 0, the phantom take-profit on a bot buy that would revert on its 0.99 minOut once ~0.012 ETH sits ahead, any dust buy in the launch block marking a front pool late, score.py mixing front dry decisions into follow's paper stats, and a relay short of the front stake stopping the whole engine at start. Pass D (rv/, on 7d1f645) confirmed three findings fixed in 50bd0aa (41 tests): a front trade holding the slot through its 3 s pull check and an eth_getBalance that could hang 4x10 s (settle_delay old vs new; s5_verify cases A-F at 80 ms RPC/150 ms send), front yielding to a follow launch follow could not trade (cap, stop, relay below stake; v_yield), and an arming-thread start failure leaving a hash in state["arming"] that blocked every live front fire for the run (arming_leak, s4, s4b). One Pass-C fix was later deliberately relaxed: 7d1f645 made the fee/tick-spacing/price/liquidity fingerprint logged-only (FRONT_PIN=0 by default and on the box), so a copycat launch with the bot's value and calldata shape is traded again; only the sqrtP bounds, the sub-1% fee rule and the armed-once rule remain. The review of the safe-stop commit ea66cc2 (handoff 12.2/16.7) left no directory, script or note anywhere under research/session_oct9_10 and the latest HANDOFF (commit 4dce46e) still says it was running: it never completed. Test counts per commit, from the test files themselves: bd8ed8d 11, c2d9fcb 20, e24ae29 21, 5827baf 23, 48af392 29, 506a254 39, 7d1f645 39, 50bd0aa 41, 32416c1 42, cd3d1c9 43, ea66cc2 44, 3c3f696 44, 3a47c34 45, 792a8c5 46 — consistent with handoff 7.4. The scripts are not runnable as copied: 10 carry the old absolute scratch path, 31 hardcode /home/user/v2pons, most import the CURRENT engine or test file (so they now exercise fixed code or break on changed markers), and every intermediate data file they read or write (newsch.json, react_rows.json, found.json, pools.json, res.pkl, frames.txt, window_out.json, out/*, others/census.jsonl) is absent.
